 def Indist (X Y : Ensemble ℓ) : Prop :=
   ∀ D : NUPPT, Negligible fun n => distAdv (X.X n) (Y.X n) (D.distinguisher n (ℓ.eval n))

+/-- **The inversion experiment of `g` on split strings** [PS10, Theorem 87.3]: the inversion
+probability of `g = glPad` against `M` at `n` is that of the concrete
+`appendEquiv ∘ HardcoreBit.glPad (f n) ∘ appendEquiv⁻¹` on `{0,1}^{inLen(n) + inLen(n)}`, the
+inputs read through `glPad_inEquiv` and the outputs through `glPad_outEquiv`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Reindex the uniform input by `glPad_inEquiv⁻¹` (`invProbAt_eq_uprob`,
+`invProbOpt_def`, `uprob_comp_equiv`); `g` is the concrete map read through the two equivalences
+(by definition), the encodings agree (`glPad_inEquiv_ofFn`, `glPad_inEquiv_symm_ofFn`,
+`glPad_outEquiv_symm_ofFn`), and preimages correspond since both equivalences and
+`Fin.appendEquiv` are injective. -/
+private theorem invProbAt_glPad_eq (M : NUPPT) (n : ℕ) :
+    𝓕.glPad.invProbAt M n =
+      invProbOpt (fun z : Fin (𝓕.inLen.eval n + 𝓕.inLen.eval n) → Bool =>
+        Fin.appendEquiv (𝓕.outLen.eval n) (𝓕.inLen.eval n)
+          (HardcoreBit.glPad (𝓕.f n) ((Fin.appendEquiv (𝓕.inLen.eval n) (𝓕.inLen.eval n)).symm z)))
+        (M.inverter n (𝓕.inLen.eval n + 𝓕.inLen.eval n) (𝓕.outLen.eval n + 𝓕.inLen.eval n)) := by
+  have hf : ∀ v, 𝓕.glPad.f n v = (𝓕.glPad_outEquiv n).symm (Fin.appendEquiv _ _
+      (HardcoreBit.glPad (𝓕.f n) ((Fin.appendEquiv _ _).symm (𝓕.glPad_inEquiv n v)))) :=
+    fun _ => rfl
+  rw [FunFamily.invProbAt_eq_uprob, invProbOpt_def,
+    ← uprob_comp_equiv ((𝓕.glPad_inEquiv n).symm.prodCongr (Equiv.refl (M.Coins n)))]
+  refine OneWay.uprob_congr fun z => ?_
+  simp only [Equiv.prodCongr_apply, Equiv.coe_refl, Prod.map_fst, Prod.map_snd, id_eq, hf,
+    Equiv.apply_symm_apply, NUPPT.inverter_eq_some_iff, glPad_outEquiv_symm_ofFn]
+  constructor
+  · rintro ⟨x', hx', hfx⟩
+    refine ⟨𝓕.glPad_inEquiv n x', by rw [hx', glPad_inEquiv_ofFn], ?_⟩
+    exact (Fin.appendEquiv _ _).symm.injective ((𝓕.glPad_outEquiv n).symm.injective hfx
+      |> congrArg (Fin.appendEquiv _ _).symm)
+  · rintro ⟨x', hx', hfx⟩
+    refine ⟨(𝓕.glPad_inEquiv n).symm x', by rw [hx', glPad_inEquiv_symm_ofFn], ?_⟩

 # The Yao reduction machine

-`TCSlib.Cryptography.Asymptotic.OneWay`, which imports this file): given an inverter `A'` of the
-direct product `f'(x₁, …, x_m) = (f(x₁), …, f(x_m))`, the reduction repeats `T + 1` times —

+direct product `f'(x₁, …, x_m) = (f(x₁), …, f(x_m))`, each of `T + 1` rounds samples a position `i ←
+[m]` by rejection (D10) and `m` fresh inputs, plants the challenge `y` at block `i`, calls `A'` and

+/-- **Averaging a favourable mass**: if `q` is nonnegative and at least `b` on every output
+satisfying `P`, then `b * prob c P ≤ expect c q`. Glue, no textbook counterpart of its own;
+first use: the averaging step of [PS10, proof of Theorem 137.2]. It is the `ProbComp`
+counterpart of `HardcoreBit.uprob_mul_le_uexp`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Write the probability as the expectation of the indicator of `P`, move `b`
+inside, and compare pointwise: on `P` the integrand `b` is at most `q`, off it `0 ≤ q`. -/
+theorem mul_prob_le_expect (c : ProbComp α) (P : α → Prop) (q : α → ℝ) (b : ℝ)
+    (hq : ∀ a, 0 ≤ q a) (hP : ∀ a, P a → b ≤ q a) :
+    b * prob c P ≤ expect c q := by
+  classical
+  rw [prob_eq_expect, ← expect_const_mul]
+  apply expect_mono
+  intro a
+  by_cases ha : P a
+  · simpa only [ha, if_true, mul_one] using hP a ha
+  · simpa only [ha, if_false, mul_zero] using hq a
+

 Two implementations of the same oracles may keep states of different types: in a security proof
 the real game and its simulation often record different data for the same history (for BB1, the
 committed identity with a group element `h`, or with an exponent `α_1`). If a relation `R`
-between the two kinds of state holds at the start, and every call from related states answers
-alike and leads to related states, then the two simulations of any computation answer alike and
-end in related states (`ProbComp.expect_simulate_rel`). "Alike" is measured by expectations: a
-call, or a run, from related states gives the same expectation to any two functions of the
-answer and the state that agree at equal answers and related states.
+between the two kinds of state holds at the start, and every call from related states gives
+equal expectations to any two tests of the answer and the new state that agree at equal answers
+and related states, then so does every simulated computation, for tests of the output and the
+final state (`ProbComp.expect_simulate_rel`). This preserves equality of compatible test
+expectations; it is not a coupling of the two runs supported on `R`. A coupling of each call's
+outcomes supported on equal answers and related states gives the per-call hypothesis
+(`ProbComp.expect_sample_rel` for one uniform sample), but for an arbitrary relation the
+hypothesis does not make the final states coupled inside `R` (Gate 49, G49-01). In particular
+the output laws agree (`ProbComp.prob_simulate_rel`).

+  cases List.find? (fun i => spAcc (spStep^[i] (pairEncode (List.replicate n true) (List.ofFn r))))
+      (List.range (PolyExpr.var.eval (pairEncode (List.replicate n true) (List.ofFn r)).length + 1))
+    <;> exact id
 
 end TCSlib.Cryptography.NumberTheory

   fun w => (f w.1, w.2, B w)
